\displaystyle\frac{(\mathcal{C}^{s}_{+-}+i\mathcal{C}^{ps}_{+-})(i-\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{--}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{--})+(\mathcal{C}^{s}_{--}+i\mathcal{C}^{ps}_{--})(\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{+-}-\mathcal{C}^{v}_{+-})}{-|\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{+-}-\mathcal{C}^{v}_{+-}|^{2}+(-i+\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{++}-\mathcal{C}^{v}_{++})(\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{--}-\mathcal{C}^{v}_{--}-i)},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i(\mathcal{C}^{ps}_{++}+i\mathcal{C}^{s}_{++})(-i+\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{--}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{--})+(-i\mathcal{C}^{ps}_{-+}+\mathcal{C}^{s}_{-+})(\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{+-}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{+-})}{|\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{+-}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{+-}|^{2}-(-i+\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{--}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{--})(\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{++}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{++}-i)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i(\mathcal{C}^{ps}_{--}+i\mathcal{C}^{s}_{--})(-i+\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{++}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{++})+(-i\mathcal{C}^{ps}_{+-}+\mathcal{C}^{s}_{+-})(\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{-+}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{-+})}{|\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{+-}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{+-}|^{2}-(-i+\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{--}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{--})(\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{++}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{++}-i)},

(238)

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i(\mathcal{C}^{ps}_{-+}+i\mathcal{C}^{s}_{-+})(-i+\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{++}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{++})+(-i\mathcal{C}^{ps}_{++}+\mathcal{C}^{s}_{++})(\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{-+}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{-+})}{|\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{+-}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{+-}|^{2}-(-i+\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{--}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{--})(\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{++}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{++}-i)},

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i(\mathcal{C}^{ps}_{+-}+i\mathcal{C}^{s}_{+-})(-i+\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{--}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{--})+(-i\mathcal{C}^{ps}_{--}+\mathcal{C}^{s}_{--})(\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{+-}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{+-})}{|\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{+-}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{+-}|^{2}-(-i+\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{--}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{--})(\mathcal{C}^{pv}_{++}+\mathcal{C}^{v}_{++}-i)}.

D.2
Rank-2 conditions

The above definitions assume the denominators do not vanish. This is satisfied when the
{\cal C}^{\scriptscriptstyle A}_{ij}
are all hermitian, as shown in equation (
3.1.3
) and the text directly after it.

Conditions for rank-two boundary conditions

We next ask what is required to ensure that
\text{rank}(\mathcal{B})=2
. When this is true one pair of linearly independent columns of
\mathcal{B}
can be written as a linear combination of the other two linearly independent columns. When the
{\cal C}^{\scriptscriptstyle A}_{ij}
are hermitian we’ve seen the linearly independent pairs consist of the first and third columns of
\mathcal{B}
and the second and fourth columns of
{\cal B}
. Consequently there exist nonzero coefficients
A_{1}
,
A_{3}
,
B_{1}
and
B_{3}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{B}_{11}&\mathcal{B}_{21}&\mathcal{B}_{31}&\mathcal{B}_{41}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{1}\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{B}_{12}&\mathcal{B}_{22}&\mathcal{B}_{32}&\mathcal{B}_{42}\end{pmatrix}+B_{1}\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{B}_{14}&\mathcal{B}_{24}&\mathcal{B}_{34}&\mathcal{B}_{44}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{B}_{13}&\mathcal{B}_{23}&\mathcal{B}_{33}&\mathcal{B}_{43}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{3}\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{B}_{12}&\mathcal{B}_{22}&\mathcal{B}_{32}&\mathcal{B}_{42}\end{pmatrix}+B_{3}\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{B}_{14}&\mathcal{B}_{24}&\mathcal{B}_{34}&\mathcal{B}_{44}\end{pmatrix},

(239)

The required coefficients are given by

A_{1}=\frac{\left|\begin{array}[]{c c}\mathcal{B}_{21}&\mathcal{B}_{24}\\
\mathcal{B}_{41}&\mathcal{B}_{44}\end{array}\right|}{\left|\begin{array}[]{c c}\mathcal{B}_{22}&\mathcal{B}_{24}\\
\mathcal{B}_{42}&\mathcal{B}_{44}\end{array}\right|},\quad B_{1}=\frac{\left|\begin{array}[]{c c}\mathcal{B}_{22}&\mathcal{B}_{21}\\
\mathcal{B}_{42}&\mathcal{B}_{41}\end{array}\right|}{\left|\begin{array}[]{c c}\mathcal{B}_{22}&\mathcal{B}_{24}\\
\mathcal{B}_{42}&\mathcal{B}_{44}\end{array}\right|},\quad A_{3}=\frac{\left|\begin{array}[]{c c}\mathcal{B}_{23}&\mathcal{B}_{24}\\
\mathcal{B}_{43}&\mathcal{B}_{44}\end{array}\right|}{\left|\begin{array}[]{c c}\mathcal{B}_{22}&\mathcal{B}_{24}\\
\mathcal{B}_{42}&\mathcal{B}_{44}\end{array}\right|},\quad B_{3}=\frac{\left|\begin{array}[]{c c}\mathcal{B}_{22}&\mathcal{B}_{23}\\
\mathcal{B}_{42}&\mathcal{B}_{43}\end{array}\right|}{\left|\begin{array}[]{c c}\mathcal{B}_{22}&\mathcal{B}_{24}\\
\mathcal{B}_{42}&\mathcal{B}_{44}\end{array}\right|}.

(240)

Comparing these solutions to (
235
) implies

A_{1}=-\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}},\quad B_{1}=-{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}e^{-i{\mathfrak{a}}},\quad A_{3}=-{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}e^{i{\mathfrak{a}}},\quad B_{3}=-\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}\,,

(241)

and using these to trade
A_{1,3},B_{1,3}
for the
{\cal T}_{{\rm in}}
’s in equation (
239
) gives four tautologies as well as the following four complex conditions that can be regarded as conditions required if the matrix
{\cal B}
is to have rank 2:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{13}+{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{12}+\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{14}=0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{11}+\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{12}+{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{14}=0,

(242)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{3}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{33}+{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{32}+\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{34}=0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{31}+\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{32}+{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{34}=0.

These four complex equations amount to eight real conditions. Four of these state

\Re(\mathcal{E}_{2})=0,\quad\Re(\mathcal{E}_{3})=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}^{*}_{4}=0,

(243)

and, once expressions (
235
) are used, can be written purely in terms of the
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm in}}
which must therefore satisfy

\displaystyle\Re(\mathcal{E}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(|\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}+|{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}-1)=0,

\displaystyle\Re(\mathcal{E}_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(|\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}+|{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}-1)=0,

(244)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}^{*}_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}{\cal T}^{-+\,*}_{{\rm in}}+{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{++\,*}_{{\rm in}})=0,

as used in the main text. In deriving the above expressions we use the identity (
55
), which follows when the boundary coupling constants are hermitian. The relations (
244
) simply express the unitarity of the
S
matrix since the first two of these equations impose that the fermion number density of the
in
modes is conserved during scattering processes, while the third imposes that the off-diagonal matrix elements of
S^{\dagger}S
vanish.

The remaining four rank
(\mathcal{B})=2
conditions within (
242
) are

\mathcal{E}_{1}=0,\quad\Im(\mathcal{E}_{2})=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\Im(\mathcal{E}_{3})=0.

(245)

These depend on more than just the
{\cal T}_{{\rm in}}
amplitudes but can be expressed in terms of both
{\cal T}_{{\rm in}}
and
{\cal T}_{{\rm out}}
. To see why the rank-two conditions (
242
) can be written entirely in terms of
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm in}},\mathcal{T}_{{\rm out}}
take the following linear combinations

\displaystyle-\left|\begin{array}[]{ll}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{11}&\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{13}\\
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{31}&\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{33}\end{array}\right|^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{33}\,\mathcal{E}_{1}-\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{13}\,\mathcal{E}_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}+{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}=0,

\displaystyle\left|\begin{array}[]{ll}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{11}&\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{13}\\
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{31}&\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{33}\end{array}\right|^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{31}\,\mathcal{E}_{1}-\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{11}\,\mathcal{E}_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}+{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}-1=0,

\displaystyle\left|\begin{array}[]{ll}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{11}&\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{13}\\
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{31}&\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{33}\end{array}\right|^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{31}\,\mathcal{E}_{2}-\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{11}\,\mathcal{E}_{4}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}+\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}=0,

\displaystyle-\left|\begin{array}[]{ll}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{11}&\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{13}\\
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{31}&\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{33}\end{array}\right|^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{33}\,\mathcal{E}_{2}-\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{13}\,\mathcal{E}_{4}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}+\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}-1=0.

where we again use
\left|\begin{array}[]{ll}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{11}&\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{13}\\
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{31}&\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{33}\end{array}\right|\neq 0
. These can be solved to give the
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm out}}
amplitudes in terms of
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm in}}
amplitudes and vice versa, giving

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}=\frac{{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}}{\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}-\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}},\qquad\,\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}=-\frac{\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}}{\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}-\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}=-\frac{\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}}{\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}-\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}},\qquad\,{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}=\frac{{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}}{\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}-\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}}

(258)

and

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}=\frac{{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}}{\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}-\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}},\qquad\,\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}=-\frac{\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}}{\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}-\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}=-\frac{\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}}{\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}-\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}},\qquad\,{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}=\frac{{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}}{\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}-\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}}.

(259)

Modulus and phase of
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm in}}
and
{\cal T}_{{\rm out}}

The constraint conditions (
244
) imply that the four complex
{\cal T}_{{\rm in}}
amplitudes satisfy four real constraints and so actually only contain four independent real parameters.
To see why notice that the first two conditions in (
244
) can be used to write the general amplitudes as

\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}=\rho_{+}\,e^{i\theta_{++}},\quad{\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}=\sqrt{1-\rho_{+}^{2}}\,e^{i\theta_{-+}},\quad\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}=\rho_{-}\,e^{i\theta_{--}},\quad\text{and}\quad{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}=\sqrt{1-\rho_{-}^{2}}\,e^{i\theta_{+-}},

(260)

for the six real parameters
\rho_{\pm}\geq 0
and
\theta_{++},\theta_{+-},\theta_{-+},\theta_{--}
. Plugging these expressions into the third condition in (
244
) then gives

\rho_{-}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}_{+}}\,e^{i(\theta_{--}-\theta_{-+})}=\rho_{+}\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}_{-}}\,e^{-i(\theta_{++}-\theta_{+-}+\pi)},

(261)

and so taking the modulus squared of each side of the equation shows
\rho_{+}=\rho_{-}
. Additionally, the four phases are not independent because they satisfy

\theta_{++}+\theta_{--}-(\theta_{-+}+\theta_{+-})+\pi=2\pi n,

(262)

where
n
is an integer. Consequently one of the phases can be eliminated in favor of the other three. We see that the most general form of the
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm in}}
amplitudes for a hermitian theory with a rank 2 boundary matrix is then

\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}=\rho\,e^{i\theta_{++}},\quad\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}=\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\,e^{i\theta_{+-}},\quad\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}=\rho\,e^{i\theta_{--}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}=-\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\,e^{i(\theta_{++}+\theta_{--}-\theta_{+-})}.

(263)

Notice that these imply
|{\cal T}_{\rm in}^{++}|^{2}=|{\cal T}_{\rm in}^{--}|^{2}
and
|{\cal T}_{\rm in}^{+-}|^{2}=|{\cal T}_{\rm in}^{-+}|^{2}
and that the denominator appearing in (
258
) is a pure phase:

{\cal T}_{{\rm in}}^{-+}{\cal T}_{{\rm in}}^{+-}-{\cal T}_{{\rm in}}^{++}{\cal T}_{{\rm in}}^{--}=-e^{i(\theta_{++}+\theta_{--})}\,.

(264)

Implications for
{\cal T}_{{\rm out}}

Plugging the most general form (
263
) for
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm in}}
into (
258
) and using (
264
) gives

\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}=\rho\,e^{-i\theta_{++}}=(\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}})^{*},\quad\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}=\rho\,e^{-i\theta_{--}}=(\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm in}})^{*},

(265)

and

\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}=-\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\,e^{i(\theta_{+-}-\theta_{++}-\theta_{--})}=(\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}})^{*},\quad\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}=\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\,e^{-i\theta_{+-}}=(\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}})^{*}.

(266)

It is clear that the unitarity conditions satisfied by
{\cal T}_{{\rm in}}
are also satisfied by the
\mathcal{T}_{{\rm out}}
amplitudes:

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}|^{2}=|\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}|^{2}=1-|\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}|^{2}=1-|\mathcal{T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}|^{2},

\displaystyle\qquad\mathcal{T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}{\cal T}^{-+\,*}_{{\rm out}}+{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}\mathcal{T}^{++\,*}_{{\rm out}}=0.

(267)

Appendix E
Scattering states

In this appendix, we list some useful properties of the
S
-wave
in
and
out
states defined in the main text and derive the Bogoliubov relations given in §
6
. Additionally, we calculate the probability for a plane-wave state to be found in an
S
-wave,
p_{s}
, and derive the cross-section formulas in §
5
and §
6
.

E.1
Properties of
in
and
out
states

Orthogonality and normalization relations

The unitarity constraints on
in
amplitudes (
73
)-(
74
) as well as the analogous
out
amplitude constraints (
83
)-(
84
) can be used to show that the
in
and
out
modes satisfy the orthogonality and normalization relations

\int^{\infty}_{\epsilon}\mathrm{d}r\,({\mathfrak{u}}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\dagger}\,{\mathfrak{u}}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\int^{\infty}_{\epsilon}\mathrm{d}r\,({\mathfrak{v}}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\dagger}\,{\mathfrak{v}}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=2\pi\delta(k-k^{\prime})\,\delta_{{\mathfrak{s}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}},

(268)

and

\int^{\infty}_{\epsilon}\mathrm{d}r\,({\mathfrak{u}}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\dagger}\,{\mathfrak{v}}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=2\pi\delta(k+k^{\prime})\,\delta_{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{s^{\prime}}}.

(269)

where the label d indicates the direction of motion
i.e.

{\text{d}}=\{{\rm in},{\rm out}\}
. These relations can be used to show that, when the
S
-wave fermion field is expanded in terms of
in
,
out
modes as

\displaystyle\bm{\chi}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathfrak{s}}=\pm}\int^{\infty}_{0}\frac{{\rm d}k}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\Bigl[{\mathfrak{u}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}(x)\,a^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}+{\mathfrak{v}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}(x)\,({\overline{a}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\Bigr],

(270)

the particle and antiparticle creation and annihilation operator anticommutation relations are given by

\Bigl\{{a}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k},({a}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime},k^{\prime}})^{\star}\Bigr\}=\Bigl\{{\overline{{a}}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k},({\overline{{a}}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime},k^{\prime}})^{\star}\Bigr\}=\delta(k-k^{\prime})\,\delta_{{\mathfrak{s}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}},

(271)

with all other anticommutators vanishing.

For some applications, it is preferable to consider discretely normalized states. We define the discretely normalized
in
,
out
modes as

\bm{u}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}(x)\coloneqq\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2L}}{\mathfrak{u}}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}(x),\quad\bm{v}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}(x)\coloneqq\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2L}}{\mathfrak{v}}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}(x),

(272)

since the orthogonality and normalization relations for these modes become

\displaystyle\int^{L+\epsilon}_{\epsilon}\mathrm{d}r\,(\bm{u}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\dagger}\,\bm{u}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\int^{L+\epsilon}_{\epsilon}\mathrm{d}r\,(\bm{v}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\dagger}\,\bm{v}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\delta_{kk^{\prime}}\,\delta_{{\mathfrak{s}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}},

(273)

and

\int^{L+\epsilon}_{\epsilon}\mathrm{d}r\,(\bm{u}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\dagger}\,\bm{v}^{\text{d}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\delta_{k,-k^{\prime}}\,\delta_{{\mathfrak{s}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}},

(274)

in the large
L
limit. The
S
-wave fermion field can be expanded in terms of the discretely normalized bases:

\displaystyle\bm{\chi}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathfrak{s}}=\pm}\sum^{\infty}_{k\geq 0}\,\Bigl[\bm{u}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}(x)\,\bm{a}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}+\bm{v}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}(x)\,({\overline{\bm{a}}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\Bigr],

(275)

where the discrete normalization particle and antiparticle creation and annihilation operators satisfy

\Bigl\{\bm{a}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k},(\bm{a}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime},k^{\prime}})^{\star}\Bigr\}=\Bigl\{{\overline{\bm{a}}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k},({\overline{\bm{a}}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime},k^{\prime}})^{\star}\Bigr\}=\delta_{kk^{\prime}}\,\delta_{{\mathfrak{s}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}},

(276)

with all other anticommutators vanishing.

Bogoliubov relations

The
in
creation and annihilation operators can be expanded in terms of the corresponding
out
operators and vice versa, as in the Bogoliubov relations (
140
) and (
141
). To see this, note that the
in
operators satisfy

a^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{8\pi}}\int^{\infty}_{-\infty}{\rm d}t\,({\mathfrak{u}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}(r,t))^{\dagger}\bm{\chi}(r,t),\quad\text{and}\quad({\overline{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{8\pi}}\int^{\infty}_{-\infty}{\rm d}t\,({\mathfrak{v}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}(r,t))^{\dagger}\bm{\chi}(r,t),

(277)

which can be shown by expanding
\bm{\chi}(x)
in terms of the
in
basis. Since
{\bm{\chi}}(x)
can equivalently be expanded in terms of
out
states, the above equations imply that
e.g.

a^{{\rm in}}_{+,k}
is given by

\displaystyle a^{{\rm in}}_{+,k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{8\pi}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}=\pm}\int^{\infty}_{-\infty}{\rm d}t\,\int^{\infty}_{0}\frac{{\rm d}k^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}({\mathfrak{u}}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k}(r,t))^{\dagger}\,\left({\mathfrak{u}}^{\rm out}_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(x)\,a^{\rm out}_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}+{\mathfrak{v}}^{\rm out}_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(x)\,({\overline{a}}^{\rm out}_{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime},k^{\prime}})^{\star}\right)

(278)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int^{\infty}_{0}{{\rm d}k^{\prime}}\,\Big({\cal T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}e^{2ik\epsilon}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{r_{0}}\right)^{-ieQ}\,\delta(\omega_{+,k}-\omega_{+,k^{\prime}})\,a^{{\rm out}}_{+,k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\,{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}e^{i(2k-ev)\epsilon}e^{i{\mathfrak{a}}}\,\left[\delta(\omega_{+,k}-\omega_{-,k^{\prime}})\,a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k^{\prime}}+\delta(\omega_{+,k}+{\overline{\omega}}_{-,k^{\prime}})\,({\overline{a}}^{{\rm out}}_{-,k^{\prime}})^{\star}\right]\Big),

where
\omega_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}=k-{\mathfrak{s}}ev/2
(
{\overline{\omega}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}=k+{\mathfrak{s}}ev/2
) is the energy of a particle (antiparticle) with quantum numbers
{\mathfrak{s}},k
. The above equation reduces to one of the Bogoliubov relations, once the integral over momentum is evaluated:

\displaystyle a^{{\rm in}}_{+,k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}\,a^{{\rm out}}_{+,k}+{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}e^{i{\mathfrak{a}}}\,\left[\Theta(k-ev)\,a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k-ev}+\Theta(-k+ev)\,({\overline{a}}^{{\rm out}}_{-,-k+ev})^{\star}\right],

(279)

where we drop powers of
k\epsilon
and
ev\epsilon
, shift
{\mathfrak{a}}
and rephase the
out
operators to absorb the
r_{0}
-dependent phase, as in the main text. The remaining Bogoliubov relations can be derived in a similar way.

It is sometimes convenient to write the Bogoliubov relations as in the last line of (
278
) since this shows that they are consistent with energy conservation, which is enforced through the delta functions that appear after the time integral is performed. Specifically, this form is useful for evaluating scattering amplitudes such as
\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,(a^{{\rm in}}_{+,k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}
, which can be written as:

\displaystyle\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,(a^{{\rm in}}_{+,k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{+-\,*}_{{\rm out}}e^{-i{\mathfrak{a}}}\int^{\infty}_{0}{\rm d}p\,\delta(\omega_{+,k}-\omega_{-,p})\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,(a^{{\rm out}}_{-,p})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}

(280)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{+-\,*}_{{\rm out}}\braket{0_{{\rm out}}|0_{{\rm in}}}e^{-i{\mathfrak{a}}}\Theta(k-ev)\delta(\omega_{+,k}-\omega_{-,k^{\prime}}),

where in the last line we use the ‘momentum-conserving’ delta function
\delta(p-k^{\prime})
implicit in the overlap
\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,(a^{{\rm out}}_{-,p})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}
to perform the integral over
p
. The surviving delta function is the original energy-conserving one from (
278
) which is regularized by a factor of the duration of the interaction,
T
, when probabilities are calculated.

The above argument shows that the Heisenberg picture transition probabilities (which can be obtained from the amplitudes listed in §
6
) depend on
T
in the same way as their interaction picture counterparts, and so rates and cross sections can be defined in much the same way in both pictures. This can also be seen in a simpler way, by re-evaluating the amplitude
\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,(a^{{\rm in}}_{+,k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}
using the ‘standard’ form of the relevant Bogoliubov relation, (
279
), in the following way

\displaystyle\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,(a^{{\rm in}}_{+,k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{+-\,*}_{{\rm out}}e^{-i{\mathfrak{a}}}\,\Theta(k-ev)\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,(a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k-ev})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}

(281)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{+-\,*}_{{\rm out}}\braket{0_{{\rm out}}|0_{{\rm in}}}e^{-i{\mathfrak{a}}}\,\Theta(k-ev)\delta(k-k^{\prime}-ev)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal T}^{+-\,*}_{{\rm out}}\braket{0_{{\rm out}}|0_{{\rm in}}}e^{-i{\mathfrak{a}}}\,\Theta(k-ev)\delta(\omega_{+,k}-\omega_{-,k^{\prime}}),

where the delta function in the second line comes from the overlap
\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,(a^{{\rm out}}_{-,k-ev})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}
and is rewritten in the third line by performing a change of variables from momentum to energy. Since the final expression in (
281
) involves an energy-conserving delta function, we can regulate it with a factor of
T
per the usual procedure.

Expectation values of energy and electric, axial charge

The
in
and
out
states are eigenstates of the
S
-wave fermionic hamiltonian (with
\widehat{A}_{0}=0
):

H_{\scriptscriptstyle F}=\frac{1}{2}\Bigl[{\overline{\bm{\chi}}}\,O_{{\cal B}}({\mathfrak{a}})\,\bm{\chi}\Bigr]_{r=\epsilon,t}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{{\mathfrak{s}}=\pm}\int^{\infty}_{\epsilon}{\rm d}r\,{\overline{{\chi}}}_{{\mathfrak{s}}}\left[\Gamma^{1}\overset{\leftrightarrow}{\partial}_{1}-i{\mathfrak{s}}\,\Gamma^{0}\left(ev-\frac{eQ}{r}\right)\right]\chi_{{\mathfrak{s}}},

(282)

when
{\mathfrak{a}}
is treated as a classical variable within the Born-Oppenheimer approximation. This can be seen from the expansion of
H_{\scriptscriptstyle F}
in terms of
in
or
out
states (see appendix
G
for details of how calculations involving fermion bilinears are done)

\displaystyle H_{\scriptscriptstyle F}

\displaystyle=E^{\text{d}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle F}}+\sum_{{\mathfrak{s}}=\pm}\int^{\infty}_{0}{\rm d}k\,\left[\left(k-\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}ev\right)(a^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\,a^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}+\left(k+\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}ev\right)({\overline{a}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\,{\overline{a}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\right],

(283)

which implies that, relative to the vacuum, the single particle and antiparticle states have energies

\omega_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}=k-\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}ev\quad\text{and}\quad{\overline{\omega}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}=k+\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}ev,

(284)

where
\omega_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}
(
{\overline{\omega}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}
) is the energy of the particle state
(a^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{\text{d}}}
(antiparticle state
({\overline{a}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{\text{d}}}
) relative to the
\ket{0_{\text{d}}}
vacuum. The energies of the
in
and
out
vacuum are given by
E^{\text{d}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle F}}\coloneqq\bra{0_{\text{d}}}H_{\scriptscriptstyle F}\ket{0_{\text{d}}}
.

Generally, the
in
and
out
states are not eigenstates of the fermion electric charge
{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle F}
or of the axial charge operator
{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}
. To see this, we expand
{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle F},{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}
in terms of the
in
basis:

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle F}=

\displaystyle Q^{{\rm in}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle F}}+|\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}\int^{\infty}_{0}{\mathrm{d}k}\,\sum_{\mathfrak{s}=\pm}\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{s}e\,\Big(({a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\star}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}-(\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\star}\,\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}\Big)

(285)

\displaystyle+|\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}\,\sum_{\mathfrak{s}=\pm}\,\mathfrak{s}e\int^{\infty}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}k}{2\pi}\,PV\Bigg[\int^{\infty}_{0}\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}\Bigg(({a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\star}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k^{\prime}}\,\frac{ie^{i(k-k^{\prime})(\epsilon+t)}}{k-k^{\prime}}+({a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\star}\,(\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\frac{ie^{i(k+k^{\prime})(\epsilon+t)}}{k+k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 156.49014pt+\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}{a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k^{\prime}}\,\frac{ie^{-i(k+k^{\prime})(\epsilon+t)}}{-k-k^{\prime}}-(\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}\,\frac{ie^{-i(k-k^{\prime})(\epsilon+t)}}{-k+k^{\prime}}\Bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+e\,{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{++\,*}_{{\rm in}}e^{i{\mathfrak{a}}}\int^{\infty}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}k\,\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}}{2\pi}\underset{\beta\rightarrow 0+}{\lim}\,\Bigg[({a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k})^{\star}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,\frac{ie^{i(k-k^{\prime}-ev)t}e^{i(k-k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{k^{\prime}-k+ev+i\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+({a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k})^{\star}\,(\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\frac{ie^{i(k+k^{\prime}-ev)t}e^{i(k+k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{-k^{\prime}-k+ev+i\beta}+\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,\frac{ie^{i(-k-k^{\prime}-ev)t}e^{-i(k+k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{k^{\prime}+k+ev+i\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 85.35826pt-(\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k}\frac{ie^{i(-k+k^{\prime}-ev)t}e^{-i(k-k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{-k^{\prime}+k+ev+i\beta}\Bigg]

\displaystyle-e\,{\cal T}^{+-\,*}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}e^{-i{\mathfrak{a}}}\int^{\infty}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}k\,\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}}{2\pi}\underset{\beta\rightarrow 0+}{\lim}\Bigg[({a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k})^{\star}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k^{\prime}}\,\frac{ie^{-i(k^{\prime}-k-ev)t}e^{i(k-k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{-k^{\prime}+k+ev-i\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+({a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k})^{\star}\,(\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\frac{ie^{-i(-k^{\prime}-k-ev)t}e^{i(k+k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{k^{\prime}+k+ev-i\beta}+\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k^{\prime}}\frac{ie^{-i(k^{\prime}+k-ev)t}e^{-i(k+k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{-k^{\prime}-k+ev-i\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 85.35826pt-(\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k}\,\frac{ie^{-i(-k^{\prime}+k-ev)t}e^{-i(k-k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{k^{\prime}-k+ev-i\beta}\Bigg],

and

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}=

\displaystyle Q^{{\rm in}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle A}}+|\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}\int^{\infty}_{0}{\mathrm{d}k}\,\sum_{\mathfrak{s}=\pm}\mathfrak{s}\,\Big(({a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\star}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}-(\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\star}\,\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}\Big)

(286)

\displaystyle+\,2|\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}\,\sum_{\mathfrak{s}=\pm}\,\mathfrak{s}\int^{\infty}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}k}{2\pi}\,PV\Bigg[\int^{\infty}_{0}\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}\Bigg(({a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\star}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k^{\prime}}\,\frac{ie^{i(k-k^{\prime})(\epsilon+t)}}{k-k^{\prime}}+({a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\star}\,(\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\frac{ie^{i(k+k^{\prime})(\epsilon+t)}}{k+k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}{a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k^{\prime}}\,\frac{ie^{-i(k+k^{\prime})(\epsilon+t)}}{-k-k^{\prime}}-(\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}\,\frac{ie^{-i(k-k^{\prime})(\epsilon+t)}}{-k+k^{\prime}}\Bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle-2\,{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{++\,*}_{{\rm in}}e^{i{\mathfrak{a}}}\int^{\infty}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}k\,\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}}{2\pi}\underset{\beta\rightarrow 0+}{\lim}\Bigg[({a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k})^{\star}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,\frac{ie^{i(k-k^{\prime}-ev)t}e^{i(k-k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{k^{\prime}-k+ev+i\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+({a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k})^{\star}\,(\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\frac{ie^{i(k+k^{\prime}-ev)t}e^{i(k+k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{-k^{\prime}-k+ev+i\beta}+\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k^{\prime}}\,\frac{ie^{i(-k-k^{\prime}-ev)t}e^{-i(k+k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{k^{\prime}+k+ev+i\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 85.35826pt-(\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k}\,\frac{ie^{i(-k+k^{\prime}-ev)t}e^{-i(k-k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{-k^{\prime}+k+ev+i\beta}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+2\,{\cal T}^{+-\,*}_{{\rm in}}\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}e^{-i{\mathfrak{a}}}\int^{\infty}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}k\,\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}}{2\pi}\underset{\beta\rightarrow 0+}{\lim}\Bigg[({a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k})^{\star}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k^{\prime}}\,\frac{ie^{-i(k^{\prime}-k-ev)t}e^{i(k-k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{-k^{\prime}+k+ev-i\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+({a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k})^{\star}\,(\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\frac{ie^{-i(-k^{\prime}-k-ev)t}e^{i(k+k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{k^{\prime}+k+ev-i\beta}+\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k}\,{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k^{\prime}}\,\frac{ie^{-i(k^{\prime}+k-ev)t}e^{-i(k+k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{-k^{\prime}-k+ev-i\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 85.35826pt-(\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{+,k^{\prime}})^{\star}\,\overline{a}^{{\rm in}}_{-,k}\,\frac{ie^{-i(-k^{\prime}+k-ev)t}e^{-i(k-k^{\prime})\epsilon}}{k^{\prime}-k+ev-i\beta}\Bigg],

where
Q^{{\rm in}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle F}}\coloneqq\bra{0_{{\rm in}}}{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle F}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}
,
Q^{{\rm in}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle A}}\coloneqq\bra{0_{{\rm in}}}{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}
,
PV
refers to the Cauchy principal value of an integral and we shift
{\mathfrak{a}}
to absorb an
r_{0}
-dependent phase, as in the main text. The above expansions show that the
in
states are eigenstates of
{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle F}
only if
{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}={\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm in}}=0
,
i.e.
when only
4
D chirality-changing processes are possible at the boundary, and are eigenstates of
{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}
in the special case where
{\cal T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}={\cal T}^{--}_{{\rm in}}=0
and the boundary action only allows for charge-exchange processes. The expectation values of
{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle F},{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}
in the single particle
in
states are

\displaystyle\bra{0_{{\rm in}}}a^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle F}\,(a^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}=\left(Q^{{\rm in}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle F}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}e|\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}\right)\bra{0_{{\rm in}}}a^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,(a^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}},

\displaystyle\bra{0_{{\rm in}}}a^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}\,(a^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}=\left(Q^{{\rm in}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle A}}+{\mathfrak{s}}|\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}\right)\bra{0_{{\rm in}}}a^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,(a^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}},

(287)

and are given by

\displaystyle\bra{0_{{\rm in}}}{\overline{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle F}\,({\overline{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}=\left(Q^{{\rm in}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle F}}-\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}e|\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}\right)\bra{0_{{\rm in}}}{\overline{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,({\overline{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}},

\displaystyle\bra{0_{{\rm in}}}{\overline{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}\,({\overline{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}=\left(Q^{{\rm in}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle A}}-{\mathfrak{s}}|\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm in}}|^{2}\right)\bra{0_{{\rm in}}}{\overline{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,({\overline{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}},

(288)

for
in
states with a single antiparticle.
Out
states similarly have a definite electric charge only if
{\cal T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}={\cal T}^{-+}_{{\rm out}}=0
and a definite axial charge only when
{\cal T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}={\cal T}^{--}_{{\rm out}}=0
. For more general choices of the
{\cal T}_{{\rm out}}

out

\displaystyle\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle F}\,(a^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm out}}}=\left(Q^{{\rm out}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle F}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}e|\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}|^{2}\right)\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,(a^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm out}}},

\displaystyle\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}\,(a^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm out}}}=\left(Q^{{\rm out}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle A}}-{\mathfrak{s}}|\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}|^{2}\right)\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}a^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,(a^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm out}}},

(289)

and

\displaystyle\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}{\overline{a}}^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle F}\,({\overline{a}}^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm out}}}=\left(Q^{{\rm out}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle F}}-\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}e|\mathcal{T}^{++}_{{\rm out}}|^{2}\right)\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}{\overline{a}}^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,({\overline{a}}^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm out}}},

\displaystyle\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}{\overline{a}}^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,{\cal Q}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}\,({\overline{a}}^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm out}}}=\left(Q^{{\rm out}}_{0,{\scriptscriptstyle A}}+{\mathfrak{s}}|\mathcal{T}^{+-}_{{\rm out}}|^{2}\right)\bra{0_{{\rm out}}}{\overline{a}}^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k}\,({\overline{a}}^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm out}}}.

(290)

Equations (
287
)-(
290
) show that a measurement of the electric charge in a single-particle state
(a^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{\text{d}}}
or
({\overline{a}}^{\text{d}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{\text{d}}}
(relative to the vacuum) does not generally yield the result one would naively expect, naimely
\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}e
for particles and
-\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}e
for antiparticles. This is because the
in
and
out
states agree with the usual notion of particles only in the asymptotic past and future, respectively. More precisely, the state

(a^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}
(
({\overline{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm in}}}
) describes a particle (antiparticle) that approaches the dyon with momentum
k
, charge
\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}e
(
-\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}e
) and
4
D chirality
{\mathfrak{s}}
(-
{\mathfrak{s}}
) in the asymptotic past and then scatters either to a particle (antiparticle) with different electric charge and momentum or to one with different
4
D chirality. The
out
states
(a^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm out}}}
(
({\overline{a}}^{{\rm out}}_{{\mathfrak{s}},k})^{\star}\ket{0_{{\rm out}}}
) similarly describe particles (antiparticles) of momentum
k
, charge
\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}e
(
-\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{s}}e
) and
4
D chirality
-{\mathfrak{s}}
(
{\mathfrak{s}}
) in the asymptotic future and have a more complicated description at early times. This can be shown explicitly by writing single-particle states as the infinitesimally narrow limit of a wave-packet peaked around a given momentum, and evaluating the fermionic current expectation values in these states in the asymptotic past (for
in
states) or asymptotic future (
out
states).

E.2
S
-wave projection of plane-wave states

For scattering problems, the initial states of interest correspond to
4
D plane waves far from the dyon and so are not prepared in the
S
-wave.
We now show how these plane wave states can be projected onto the
S
-wave sector and use this to compute the cross sections for single-particle
S
-wave scattering given in the main text.

To start, we note that the Dirac equation simplifies considerably at large distances from the dyon, since the background Julia-Zee potential becomes constant
i.e.

e\mathcal{A}^{3}_{\mu}(x)\underset{r\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}ev\,\delta^{0}_{\mu}
. The full fermion field can then be expanded in terms of particle and antiparticle mode functions
U_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}(x),V_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}(x)

whose asymptotic form is equivalent to that of plane-wave spinors, up to a phase
32
32

32

Note that this phase has to be defined such that
U_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}(x),\,V_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}(x)
are sections
i.e.
it should be defined differently in the
R_{+}
and
R_{-}
region.
. The particle mode functions then satisfy

U_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}(x)\underset{r\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}{{\cal N}}\,e^{-i(k-\mathfrak{s}\,ev/2)t}e^{i\vec{k}\cdot\vec{x}}e^{i\Phi^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x)}\xi_{\mathfrak{s}}\otimes\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle U}_{\mathfrak{c}},

(291)

where
\tau_{3}\,\xi_{\mathfrak{s}}=\mathfrak{s}\,\xi_{\mathfrak{s}}
,
\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle U}_{+}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1&0&0\end{pmatrix}^{T}
,
\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle U}_{-}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&1&0\end{pmatrix}^{T}
and
{\cal N}
is a normalization factor. Similarly, the antiparticle mode functions are asymptotically given by

V_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}(x)\underset{r\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}{{\cal N}}\,e^{i(k+\mathfrak{s}\,ev/2)t}e^{-i\vec{k}\cdot\vec{x}}e^{i\Phi^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle V}(x)}\xi_{\mathfrak{s}}\otimes\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle V}_{\mathfrak{c}},

(292)

where
\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle V}_{+}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&0\end{pmatrix}^{T}
,
\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle V}_{-}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0&1\end{pmatrix}^{T}
. The functions
\Phi^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle U},{\scriptscriptstyle V}}(x)
are phases whose radial dependence is
(r/r_{0})^{i\mathfrak{s}eQ/2}

33
33

33

This choice ensures the asymptotic radial dependence of the incoming spherical waves
in
U,V
and in partial wave solutions match.

, and whose angular dependence we discuss shortly. We choose the normalization factor,
\mathcal{N}
,
such that the mode functions satisfy the following orthogonality and normalization relations

\displaystyle\int^{\infty}_{-\infty}\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,U^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}\,U_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime},\vec{k}^{\prime}}=\int^{\infty}_{-\infty}\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,V^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}\,V_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime},\vec{k}^{\prime}}=(2\pi)^{3}\delta(\vec{k}-\vec{k}^{\prime})\delta_{\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}}\delta_{\mathfrak{c}\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}},

(293)

as well as

\int^{\infty}_{-\infty}\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,U^{\dagger}_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}\,V_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime},\vec{k}^{\prime}}=0,

(294)

The full fermion field can be expanded in terms of the
U_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}(x),\,V_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}(x)
basis as follows:

\bm{\psi}(x)=\sum_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c}=\pm}\int^{\infty}_{-\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3/2}}\left(a_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}\,U_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}(x)+(\overline{a}_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}})^{\star}\,V_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}(x)\right),

(295)

where the plane-wave particle and antiparticle creation and annihilation operators satisfy

\{a_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}},(a_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime},\vec{k}^{\prime}})^{\star}\}=\{\overline{a}_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}},(\overline{a}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime},\vec{k}^{\prime}})^{\star}\}=\delta(\vec{k}-\vec{k}^{\prime})\delta_{{\mathfrak{s}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}\delta_{\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}},

(296)

with all other anticommutators being zero. We can also expand
\bm{\psi}(x)
in terms of partial wave states,

\bm{\psi}(x)=\sum_{\mathfrak{s}=\pm}\int^{\infty}_{0}\frac{\mathrm{d}k}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left({a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}\,u^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}(x)+(\overline{{a}}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k})^{\star}\,v^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s},k}(x)\right)+\sum_{j>0}\bm{\psi}_{j}(x)

(297)

where
u^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s}k}(x),v^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s}k}(x)
are the
4
D equivalents of the
2
D
in
modes
34
34

34

The
4
D
in
,
out
modes are given by (
24
) where
f_{\pm}(r,t),g_{\pm}(r,t)
are components of the corresponding
2
D basis, as in (
29
).
and
\bm{\psi}_{j}(x)
is the
j
-th fermion partial wave. Since both the plane-wave and partial wave bases are complete, we can expand the plane-wave particle creation operators
(a_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}})^{\star}
in terms of partial wave creation operators

({a}_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}})^{\star}=\sum_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}=\pm}\int^{\infty}_{0}{\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{D}^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\,({a}^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}})^{\star}+\sum_{j>0,\kappa_{j}}\mathcal{D}^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}_{j,\kappa_{j}}\,({a}_{j,\kappa_{j}})^{\star},

(298)

where
({a}_{j,\kappa_{j}})^{\star}
is a creation operator for a state in the
j
-th partial wave with quantum numbers
\kappa_{j}
. To project the plane-wave state
(a_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}})^{\star}\ket{0}
onto the
S
-wave sector we simply need to determine the coefficients
\mathcal{D}^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}
, which can be done by expanding the corresponding plane-wave mode function in terms of
S
-wave mode functions,

\displaystyle{U}_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3/2}\bra{0}\bm{\psi}(x)\,({a}_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}})^{\star}\ket{0}

(299)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,2\pi\sum_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}=\pm}\int^{\infty}_{0}{\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}}\,\mathcal{D}^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\,u^{{\rm in}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(x)+\sum_{j>0,\kappa_{j}}(2\pi)^{3/2}\bra{0}\bm{\psi}(x)\,\mathcal{D}^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},\vec{k}}_{j,\kappa_{j}}({a}_{j,\kappa_{j}})^{\star}\ket{0},

where we use the fact that the
S
-wave annihilation operators also annihilate the full vacuum,
\ket{0}
. Antiparticle states can be projected onto the lowest partial wave in much the same way.

The coefficients
\mathcal{D}^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},k\hat{z}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}

For concretness, we now focus on particle plane-wave states which approach the dyon along the
\hat{\bm{z}}
axis. As is usually done in scattering problems, we will determine the coefficients
\mathcal{D}^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},k\hat{z}}_{\mathfrak{s}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}
by matching terms containing incoming spherical waves on the left side of (
299
) to terms with
j=0
incoming spherical waves
on the right side of this equation, at large distances from the dyon. To do this, we first isolate the incoming terms in
U_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},{k}\hat{z}}
and find their asymptotic form. Using the plane wave expansion and the asymptotic form of spherical Bessel functions, we get:

\left(U_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},{k}\hat{z}}(x)\right)_{\text{inc.}}\underset{r\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}-{\cal N}\frac{e^{-ikr}}{2ikr}\,e^{-i(k-\mathfrak{s}\,ev/2)t}e^{i\Phi^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x)}\xi_{\mathfrak{s}}\otimes\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle U}_{\mathfrak{c}}\,\overset{\infty}{\underset{\ell=0}{\sum}}(2\ell+1)(-1)^{\ell}P_{\ell}(\cos\theta),

(300)

where the subscript ‘inc.’ refers to only those terms in
U_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},{k}\hat{z}}(x)
which include spherical waves that are incident on the dyon. This expression can be simplified further by noticing that
(-1)^{\ell}=P_{\ell}(-1)
and using the Legendre polynomial completeness relation
\overset{\infty}{\underset{\ell=0}{\sum}}(2\ell+1)P_{\ell}(-1)P_{\ell}(x)=2\delta(1+x)

\left(U_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},{k}\hat{z}}(x)\right)_{\text{inc.}}\underset{r\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}-{\cal N}\frac{e^{-ikr}}{ikr}\,e^{-i(k-\mathfrak{s}\,ev/2)t}\left({r}/r_{0}\right)^{i\mathfrak{s}eQ/2}e^{i\varphi^{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c}}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(\theta)}\xi_{\mathfrak{s}}\otimes\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle U}_{\mathfrak{c}}\delta(1+\cos\theta),

(301)

where we now specialize to the
R_{-}
region and rewrite
e^{i\Phi^{{\mathfrak{s}},{\mathfrak{c}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x)}
as
\left(r/r_{0}\right)^{i{\mathfrak{s}}eQ/2}e^{i\varphi^{{\mathfrak{s}},{\mathfrak{c}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(\theta)}
in this region
35
35

35

We choose the
\phi
-dependence this way since the plane-wave travels along the
+\hat{\bm{z}}
axis and so is incident on the dyon in the
R_{-}
region.

We can expand the top and bottom two components of each Dirac spinor in
U_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},{k}\hat{z}}(x)
in terms of eigensections of the total angular momentum and its third component (
J^{2}
,

)

\displaystyle\left(U_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{c},{k}\hat{z}}(x)\right)_{\text{inc.}}\underset{r\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}

\displaystyle-{\cal N}\frac{e^{-ikr}}{ikr}\,e^{-i(k-\mathfrak{s}\,ev/2)t}\left({r}/r_{0}\right)^{i\mathfrak{s}eQ/2}\xi_{\mathfrak{s}}

(302)

\displaystyle\quad\otimes\Bigg[\begin{pmatrix}A^{{\mathfrak{c}}}_{0,\mathfrak{s}}{\eta}_{\mathfrak{s}}(\theta,\phi)\\
B^{{\mathfrak{c}}}_{0,\mathfrak{s}}{\eta}_{\mathfrak{s}}(\theta,\phi)\end{pmatrix}+\overset{\infty}{\underset{j>0}{\sum}}\sum^{j}_{j_{z}=-j}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\begin{pmatrix}A^{{\mathfrak{c}},(i)}_{j,j_{z},\mathfrak{s}}\eta^{(i)}_{j,j_{z},\mathfrak{s}}(\theta,\phi)\\[2.84526pt]
B^{{\mathfrak{c}},(i)}_{j,j_{z},\mathfrak{s}}\eta^{(i)}_{j,j_{z},\mathfrak{s}}(\theta,\phi)\end{pmatrix}\Bigg],

where
{\eta}_{\mathfrak{s}}(\theta,\phi)
are defined as in equation (
25
) and the sentence below it and the higher partial wave angular momentum eigensections
\eta^{(1)}_{j,j_{z},\mathfrak{s}}(\theta,\phi)
,
\eta^{(2)}_{j,j_{z},\mathfrak{s}}(\theta,\phi)
are given by the eigensections
\phi^{(1)}_{j,j_{z}}(\theta,\phi)
,
\phi^{(2)}_{j,j_{z}}(\theta,\phi)
from
[
